纽约州宣布全州紧急状态 麻疹 病例数达到 108 例 - Baller Alert

New York Declares Statewide Measles Emergency as Cases Hit 108 - Baller Alert
新闻简报纽约州州长 Kathy Hochul 于 2026 年 10 月 6 日宣布全州进入紧急状态 麻疹,因为该州今年记录了 108 例确诊病例。其中大部分病例(92 例)是自 2026 年 7 月 15 日以来报告的,主要发生在 疫苗接种 率较低的 18 个县的农村社区。该紧急命令允许药剂师、护理人员、助产士和护士分别管理 MMR 疫苗 并进行 麻疹 测试。这一应对措施旨在加速 疫苗接种 的工作并减轻 麻疹病毒 的传播,麻疹病毒 可能导致肺炎、脑肿胀和死亡等严重并发症。暴发 是全国范围内大规模激增的一部分,宾夕法尼亚州报告了 1,000 多例病例和 5 人死亡。该州的行动旨在解决公共卫生风险,并与其保持 麻疹 无状态的目标保持一致,泛美卫生组织将于 2026 年 11 月对其进行审查。
查看新闻五要素
时间
纽约州州长 Kathy Hochul 于 2026 年 10 月 6 日宣布 麻疹 进入紧急状态,该州全年病例数攀升至 108 例。大部分传播是最近发生的,自 2026 年 7 月 15 日以来报告了 92 例病例。
地点与对象
截至 2026 年 10 月 3 日,纽约州已有 108 例确诊的 麻疹 病例。其中 7 例发生在纽约市,101 例分布在该州其他地区,特别是 麻疹 发病率较低的 18 个县的农村社区。仅刘易斯县就记录了 11 例病例。
事件
由于病例数量不断增加,纽约州于 2026 年 10 月 6 日宣布全州进入紧急状态 麻疹。州长签署了一项行政命令,允许药剂师、护理人员、助产士和护士分别管理 MMR 疫苗 和进行 麻疹 测试。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年10月3日,纽约州已确诊108例麻疹病例,自7月15日以来报告了92例。该州正在经历严重的麻疹,麻疹病毒可导致肺炎、脑肿胀和死亡等严重并发症。亚急性硬化性全脑炎 是一种罕见的并发症,可在感染数年后导致永久性脑损伤。
应对、状态与不确定性
该州已实施灾难紧急状态,以加快 疫苗 的分发和测试。自 暴发 开始以来,地方卫生部门已在农村地区注射了 1,000 多剂 MMR 疫苗。州长有权暂时更改或暂停州规则,以更有效地解决 暴发 问题。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

New York Declares Statewide Measles Emergency as Cases Hit 108 - Baller Alert

News Brief
New York Governor Kathy Hochul declared a statewide measles emergency on October 6, 2026, as the state recorded 108 confirmed cases for the year. The majority of these cases, 92, were reported since July 15, 2026, primarily in rural communities across 18 counties with low vaccination rates. The emergency order allows pharmacists, paramedics, midwives, and nurses to administer the MMR vaccine and conduct measles tests, respectively. This response aims to accelerate vaccination efforts and mitigate the spread of the measles virus, which can lead to severe complications like pneumonia, brain swelling, and death. The outbreak is part of a larger national surge, with Pennsylvania reporting over 1,000 cases and five deaths. The state’s actions are intended to address the public health risk and align with its goal of maintaining measles-free status, which will be reviewed by the Pan American Health Organization in November 2026.
Original source text (English)
​ Measles Emergency Declared in New York as Cases Hit 108 Home News Entertainment The Baller Alert Show Baller Alert Lists Baller Alert Exclusives Ballerific Music That’s Baller Fashion Metaverse Tech Lifestyle Sports Op-Ed Travel Health EVENTS Videos Shop Micro Dramas About Political News en español No Result View All Result Home News Entertainment The Baller Alert Show Baller Alert Lists Baller Alert Exclusives Ballerific Music That’s Baller Fashion Metaverse Tech Lifestyle Sports Op-Ed Travel Health EVENTS Videos Shop Micro Dramas About Political News en español No Result View All Result No Result View All Result New York Declares Statewide Measles Emergency as Cases Hit 108 Hochul's order lets pharmacists, paramedics and midwives give the MMR shot as cases climb and Pennsylvania's outbreak tops 1,000 by Grace L. October 6, 2026 in Health, News Reading Time: 3 mins read New York Declares Statewide Measles Emergency as Cases Hit 108 New York Governor Kathy Hochul declared a statewide measles emergency on Monday, signing an executive order as the state’s case count climbed to 108 for the year. Executive Order 65 puts a State Disaster Emergency in place for all of New York through November 4. A disaster emergency gives the governor power to temporarily change or suspend certain state rules so officials can move faster than the usual red tape allows. Here, that means getting more vaccines into arms and more tests done. “After reviewing the latest data on measles cases in New York State, I am declaring a State Disaster Emergency to strengthen our ongoing efforts to confront this disease,” Hochul said in a statement. As of October 3, New York had confirmed 108 measles cases in 2026. Seven are in New York City, and the other 101 are spread across the rest of the state. Most of the spread is recent. Of the 108 cases, 92 have been reported since July 15, largely in rural communities across 18 counties where vaccination rates are low. Lewis County alone has recorded 11 cases. The biggest change under the measles emergency is who can give the vaccine. Paramedics, advanced EMS providers, pharmacists and midwives can now administer the MMR shot, which protects against measles, mumps and rubella. That expanded group can vaccinate children as young as 2. Nurses can also now order measles tests, which before required a doctor. Local health departments have already given more than 1,000 MMR doses in rural areas since the outbreak picked up. The state calls the vaccine the most effective protection against the disease. Hochul’s measles emergency also comes with a crisis brewing next door. Pennsylvania had 977 cases and five deaths as of October 2, many in rural counties that border New York. This week, Pennsylvania passed 1,000 cases, making it the largest measles outbreak in the country in three decades. The last outbreak that size happened in New York City in the early 1990s and stretched over several years. New York’s order is the first declaration of its kind since measles cases began rising across the country in 2025. Measles spreads easily through the air and can lead to pneumonia, brain swelling and death. One rare complication, called subacute sclerosing panencephalitis, is brain inflammation that can show up years after someone recovers from measles and cause permanent brain damage. Outbreaks take hold when vaccination rates in a community fall below about 95 percent, the level needed to keep measles from moving person to person. The surge is happening while the nation’s top health official is Robert F. Kennedy Jr., a longtime anti vaccine activist who chaired the group Children’s Health Defense for years before Trump named him Secretary of Health and Human Services. Since taking over in 2025, Kennedy fired all 17 members of the Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices, the expert panel that guides national vaccine policy, and replaced them with vaccine skeptics. He also moved to shrink the childhood vaccine schedule, changes a judge has since halted. Kennedy has claimed the measles vaccine causes deaths “every year.” The Infectious Diseases Society of America says no deaths have been shown to be linked to the MMR vaccine in healthy people. At a congressional hearing this year, Kennedy insisted, “I have never been anti-vaccine,” while also claiming vaccines are not properly tested for safety. He has argued the national outbreak began in January 2025, before he took office. More recently, Kennedy has changed his tune, telling parents on CNN they should get their children vaccinated for measles and calling the shot effective. The stakes reach beyond New York. The United States declared measles eliminated in 2000, meaning the disease was no longer spreading continuously inside the country. In November, officials with the Pan American Health Organization, the regional arm of the World Health Organization, will meet to decide whether the US and Mexico keep that measles free status. New York State, with 108 confirmed measles cases as of October 3, 2026. Seven cases are in New York City, and 101 are spread across the rest of the state, particularly in rural communities across 18 counties where vaccination rates are low. Lewis County alone has recorded 11 cases.
Event
New York declared a statewide measles emergency on October 6, 2026, due to a rising number of cases. The governor signed an executive order to allow pharmacists, paramedics, midwives, and nurses to administer the MMR vaccine and conduct measles tests, respectively.
Scale, impact and risk
As of October 3, 2026, there were 108 confirmed measles cases in New York, with 92 reported since July 15. The state is experiencing a significant outbreak, and the measles virus can lead to severe complications such as pneumonia, brain swelling, and death. Subacute sclerosing panencephalitis, a rare complication, can cause permanent brain damage years after infection.
Response, status and uncertainty
